Comments
Besides the above , the following  records and registers have also been verified by  audit.
1.       Daily Bank collection register
2.       Bank Challans
3.       Abstract registers of bank receipts
4.       Bank Books
5.       Cheque issue register
6.       General Ledger
7.       Vehicle Log book
8.       Agreement with Contractor
9.       Agreement with Consultancy
10.   Connected files of planning section, Allotment section, land section,legal section
11.   Works case records
12.   Receipts books
13.   Case book of Divisions
As per Rule 100 of ODA Rule,1983 the authority shall maintain cash book including subsidiary Cash Books for  financial transaction. But it was seen that a Bank
Book is maintained instead of Cash Book in main finance. Further individual cash book is maintained by each division separately. Due to non-maintenance of the
Main Cash Book the following information could not be worked out. Hence necessary steps may be taken  to maintain the cash book instead of Bank Book.
1.       Cash analysis of closing balance
2.       Reconciliation of Bank account
3.       Details of advance position
4.       Position of fixed deposits

Comments
Bank book of Main finance maintain without opening balance and closing balance OSP-58,131-132
The bank book for the year 2014-15 has been maintained without opening balance as on 01.04.2014 and closing balance as on 31.03.2015. The bank book reflects
only the receipts and the expenditure. The audit asked the authority through objection memo to supply the Opening Balance and Closing Balance. In response to
the audit objection memo the Local Authority supplied a copy of Balance position prepared by Chartered Accountant. Authority also stated that as there is no cash
transaction BDA is not maintaining cash book including subsidiary cash book for financial transaction.  On the other hand BDA maintains Bank Book which reflects
receipts, expenditure, opening balance and closing balance of every financial year. There is also bank reconciliation process is being made regularly which
reconcile the bank account i.e. bank pass book with bank book. Hence the Local Authority is advised to mention the details of closing balances in bank book after
every day transaction.
Bank Balance not included in the Cash Book Closing Balance(Division-I) (OSP-167-168)
     On verification of the Cash Book of Division-I for the year 2014-15, it is found that the Bank balances of the concern Division were neither included in the
Opening balance as on 1.4.14 nor in the Closing balance as on 31.3.15. Three Bank accounts are being operated in different Banks & almost all the transactions
are made through cheques. The payments & receipts through Banks are only reflected in the Cash Book without affecting the Closing Balance. The CB depicts only
the advances taken by different officials which are not a part of it. As such, the actual CB as per Cash Book is not available. Only the actual balances as per the
Pass Books of different Bank Accounts has been shown in the Cash Book. Since, all the receipts & payments are being made through Bank A/Cs, the cash book
should have been balanced accordingly as per the transactions. But the same has not been followed. In response to audit objection memo the Local Authority
replied that &quot;As per prevailing practice of maintenance of cash book bank balance is not included in the closing balance of the cash book and the same is
shown separately in the cash book in each month. However the observation of LFA is noted and hence forth action is taken to include the bank balance in the
closing balance of the cash bookof this division. Hence this objection may be dropped.&quot; 
Bank Balance not included in the Cash Book Closing Balance Div-III (OSP 169-170)
     On verification of the Cash Book of Division-III for the year 2014-15, it is found that the Bank balances of the concern Division were neither included in the
Opening balance as on 1.4.14 nor in the Closing balance as on 31.3.15. Three Bank accounts are being operated in different Banks & almost all the transactions
are made through cheques. The payments & receipts through Banks are only reflected in the Cash Book without affecting the Closing Balance. The CB depicts only
the advances taken by different officials which are not a part of it. As such, the actual CB as per Cash Book is not available. Only the actual balances as per the
Pass Books of different Banks has been shown in the Cash Book. Since, all the receipts & payments are being made through Bank A/Cs, the cash book should
have been balanced accordingly as per the transactions. But the same has not been followed. Hence, the reason of such lapses in the Closing Balance of the Cash
Book may be clarified to audit. In response to the audit objection memo the local auhtority replied that &quot; This practice has already been observed since
creation of this organisation. How ever the observation of audit is noted  for necessary action in the financial year 2017-18 taking the bank balance as per pass
book to the cash book.&quot;
The reply of the Local Authority is not at all convincing and not to the point of query necessary suggestions have been given by the last and previous audit regarding
this. But no such proper step has been taken towards regularizing the maintenance of the cash book. Absence of the bank balance in the closing balance of the
cash book may lead towards financial irregularities and misutilisation of the same as it is outside the domain of the cash book.
 8 / 124

[page 9]
 AUDIT REPORT 
27-06-2017
Hence, the Local authority is suggested to look into the matter and take sincere and appropriate steps for regularization of the same. 
Bank Balance not included in the Cash Book Closing Balance Div-IV (OSP 176-194)
On checking of the Cash Book and Pass Book ( Andhra Bank and OBC ) of Division-IV for 2014-15, it was noticed that the closing balance as per cash book is
Rs103.00 as on 31.03.2015, where as closing balance as per pass book as on 31.03.2015 is 17177842.00 (OBC CB Rs16402041.00+Adhra Bank CB
Rs775801.00).All the transactions are made through Banks and the payments & receipts through Banks are only reflected in the Cash Book without affecting the
Closing Balance. Only the actual balances as per the Pass Books of different Banks has been shown in the Cash Book. Since, all the receipts & payments are
being made through Bank accounts, the cash book should have been balanced accordingly as per the transactions. But the same has not been followed. Hence,
the reason of such lapses in the Closing Balance of the Cash Book asked through audit objection memo. In response to audit objection memo the Local Authority
replied that &quot; Closing balance at the end of the month i.e. on dt31.03.2015 is Rs103.00 and Rs16402041.00 of OBC and Rs775801.00 of Andhra Bank . Total
Balance comes to Rs17177842.00 in the 2 saving account. The above said amount is reflected in the reconciliation statement but not taken in the CB as per
existing accounting procedure of BDA. Hence it is requested to drop the above para.
RECONCILIATION OF CASH BOOK & BANK PASS BOOK (Horticulture Div.) (OSP-113)
 During checking of receipt  and expenditure of cash bookw.r.t. pass book it come to notice that  bank position in pass book has not been shown in cash book .
Closing balance as per cash book as on 31.3.15 is Rs.4762.00 and closing balance as per pass book  as on 31.3.15 is Rs.202904.00. The difference was asked
through audit objection memo. In response to the audit objection memo the Local Authority replied that As per practice the OB of bank balance is not included in the
cash book. The cash position of CB Rs1042.00 is reflected in the cash book. As per pass book the CB for 2014-15 is Rs202904.00. The observation of audit is
noted for future guidance.
Comment on Maintenance of Cash Books-----
                As per the replies furnished by the Local Authority, it has been a practice to keep the bank accounts balance position outside the cash book. Neither the
Opening Balance as on 01.04.2014 nor the Closing Balance as on 31.03.2015 of the Bank position is reflected in the Cash Book. All the bank transactions are only
recorded in the Cash Book having no impact on the Closing Balance. Only cash transactions are balanced. As such the bank position is rolling on outside the
domain of the cash book. Hence the analysis of the closing balance could not be worked out. This process of maintenance of cash book is highly irregular. And
needs attention of the authority
                Hence the Local Authority should take appropriate steps towards maintenance of the Cash Book as per accounting procedure. 
Sweep Account
A sweep account is a bank  account that automatically transfer amount that exceed or fall short of a certain level into a higher interest earning investment option at
the end of each business day. Generally the excess cash is swept into money market funds. During checking of pass book it was noticed that a large number of
sweep accounts bearing different account  numbers were created and matured. But it was seen that the bank book was silent on sweep account transfer on that
day. The authority was requested through half margin memo and the information provided to audit is furnished below.

Budget 2014-15
Under Section -80 of Orissa Development Authority Act 1982 budget is prepared every year in respect of the financial year next ensuing showing the estimated
receipts and expenditure of the authority.
The budget estimate for the financial year 2014-15 has been prepared on the basis of account of the previous year i.e. 2012-13 and revised estimate for the financial
year 2013-14. The revised estimate for the year 2013-14 and budget estimate for 2014-15 was finalized vide agenda no-3 of 126 th authority meeting held on
10.06.2014 and same was sent to the commissioner-cum- secretary to Govt, Housing and Urban Development Department, Odisha Bhubaneswar vide Letter
No-24031/22.07.2014 for approval.

Comments
As per chapter-VII, rule-57 of O.D.A, Rules- 1983, physical verification of the stock of the moveable properties shall be done once in every year by such officer as
they may be authorised by the Vice-Chairman in this behalf. But , during checking the stock registers, it was noticed that the above rule was not followed by the
B.D.A. No physical verification was conducted by the authority in every year. Hence, the authority is requested to conduct the physical verification of the moveable
properties of the authority in every year and required certificate may be given in the stock register and compliance reported to audit.

Comments :
8.1 Non Adjustment of Advance of Main Finance (OSP-246-257)
AS per Rule -100 of ODA Rule 1983 advance ledger register should be maintained. The advance ledger of main finance was not maintained properly i.e.the
voucher no/date, amount, purpose of advance payment, Name of the sanctioning authority, adjustment of vouchers etc. The advance payment was booked as final
expenditure without showing in the closing balance of bank book. Similarly adjustment of advance was not shown in bank book of main finance.
While checking of the bank book w.r.t. paid vouchers for the year 2014-15 it was noticed that a sum of Rs19,85,100.00 have been paid to different employees as
advance for different purpose. The outstanding advance at the beginning of the year i.e. as on 01.04.2014 was Rs29,21,667.00.
Hence the total amount of advance came to Rs49,06,767.00 out of the same, an amount of Rs3,99,875.00 have been adjusted by adjustment vouchers
(Rs2,06,875.00 from 2013-14 and Rs1,93,000.00 from 2014-15) during the financial year 2014-15. 
As a result Rs45,06,892.00 have been remained unadjusted till 31.03.2015.
As per Finance Deptt Notification No-2221/F dtd08.03.2002 and Letter No-15179/DLFA dtd 28.09.2013 advance outstanding for more than one year need to be
surcharged from the officers who granted the same and the payee in equal proportion. The details of outstanding advance position are mentioned in the table-1,2
and 3 below.
8.2 Production of Advance ledger and outstanding advance register of Division - I (OSP-78-79):
While checking the cash book with reference to the paid vouchers of Division-I for the year 2014-15, it is noticed that a sum of Rs.126620.00 has been paid to
different officials as advance for different purpose during the year under audit. The outstanding advance at the beginning of the year i.e. as on 01.04.2014 was
Rs.33866.00. As such the total amount of advance comes to Rs.160486.00. Out of the same, an amount of Rs.121747.00 has been adjusted during the year under
audit leaving outstanding advance amount of Rs.38739.00 as on 31.03.2015. Though the balance outstanding advance against the concern officials as on
31.03.2015 has been shown in the cash book, the details of payments and the period to which the outstanding amount relates has not been reflected.

Mohanty, AEE-I
3 Sri Somanath Parida, 11856.00 114620.00 126476.00 112863.00 13613.00  
4 Total 33866.00 126620.00 160486.00 121747.00 38739.00  
Objection memo issued in this context. In response to the audit objection memo the local authority replied that “Division No-I of BDA has been entrusted with repair
and maintenance of main office building , other residential and non-residential building internal roads under the control of BDA for which provision of fund is made in
the annual budget of BDA. The BDA undertake immediate, minor/petty repair and maintenance work of urgent nature of buildings and roads by subordinate
engineering personnel. Cash payment are made for the purpose and meet such urgent requirements temporary advances allowed to the SDO,AEE, DEE in charge
of a sub division on passed Vrs which are subsequently adjusted on submission of imprest cash account to the E.E. in divisional cash book. In this connection this is
to state that total temporary advances sanctioned and paid to SDO,AEE,DEE during 2014-15 comes to Rs126620.00 with the outstanding advance of Rs33866.00
as on 01.04.2014. Advance has been adjusted to the tune of Rs121747.00 till 31.03.2015 and temporary advance laying unadjusted out of total advance paid during
2014-15 has been adjusted during 2015-16 (During Jul,2015 and Aug,2015). In this connection registers of payment of temporary advances has been maintained in
the Div-I and acknowledge obtained from the concerned officer are hereby produced to audit as requisition.
1.      The financial power delegated to the Executive Engineer of the division for engineering works is Rs5000000.00 as per delegation of power made to EE in the
132th meeting held on 20.02.2016 vide agenda item no-19/132.
2.      The petty expenses are made in connection with urgent nature of minor work and expenditure debited to the work contingency of works for which provision
exists @1% of estimates in the sanction estimate of the work.
3.      The imprest cash account along with passed vouchers are submitted to the division by SDO/AEE/DEE in charge of the sub division which are passed and
incorporated in the pass book of the division reducing outstanding advances against the SDO concerned and the monthly accounts are render to the finance
member of the BDA incorporating the receipts and expenditure.
4.      Imprest cash account are maintained by the SDO concerned in respect of the temporary advance received from the division.
5.      Temporary advance are allowed to the SDO/AEE/DEE in charge of the sub division to meet the emergent expenditure of petty works undertaken due to
exigency of the circumstances. Usually the adjustment of temporary advance is made on regular basis on receipt of imprest cash account along with vouchers but
on rare occasions adjustment of temporary advance gets delayed due to preoccupation of SDO/AEE/DEE in urgent bill work.
6.      The temporary advances are issued on passed vouchers in connection with urgent petty payments connected with urgent nature of repair and maintenance
works and temporary advance is adjusted on receipt of imprest cash accounts with vouchers. So the system is working very well and there is no necessity of
sanction of permanent advance to the SDO/AEE/DEE in charge of subdivision.
7.      Temporary advance is allowed to the SDO/AEE/DEE on passed vouchers as per provision of CPWA code.
8.      Funds to the tune of Rs10328000.00 has been received during 2014-15 from main finance towards renovation, maintenance, repairs of roads and buildings
under the control of BDA out of which 1% of estimate is earmarked for contingent expenditure. So around Rs103280.00 has been calculated to be meant for work
contingency to which petty repairs and maintenance vouchers can be booked and the contingent expenditure during 2014-15 to adjustment of temporary advance
has been made limiting to the available provision of the work contingency of sanctioned estimates of repair and maintenance work and as such no irregularity is
involve on it and the objection may be dropped.”
But the local authority has not produced any supported document in support of the adjustment. Hence the entire advance of Rs38739.00 held under objection till the
production of adjustment voucher to the next audit.. Out of which Rs326.00 needs recovery as per As per Finance Deptt Notification No-2221/F dtd08.03.2002 and
Letter No-15179/DLFA dtd 28.09.2013. Where it is mentioned that advance outstanding for more than one year need to be surcharged from the officers who granted
the same and the payee in equal proportion. The details of advance position are mentioned in the table -1,2 and 3 below.

below. The adjustment regarding the advance was asked through objection memo. In response to the audit objection memo the local authority replied that out of the

necessary action is being taken to realize the amount from rest advance holder. The adjustment documents are verified and found correct. The balance outstanding
advances are given below.
As per Finance Deptt Notification No-2221/F dtd08.03.2002 and Letter No-15179/DLFA dtd 28.09.2013 advance outstanding for more than one year need to be
surcharged from the officers who granted the same and the payee in equal proportion. The details of advance position are mentioned in the table-1,2 and 3 below.

Production of Advance ledger and outstanding advance register of Division - III (OSP-156-158):
While checking the cash book with reference to the paid vouchers of Division-III for the year 2014-15, it is noticed that a sum of Rs.197387.00 has been paid to
different officials as advance for different purpose during the year under audit. The outstanding advance at the beginning of the year i.e. as on 01.04.2014 was
Rs.258819.00. As such the total amount of advance comes to Rs.456206.00. Out of the same, an amount of Rs.365012.00 has been adjusted during the year under
audit leaving outstanding advance amount of Rs.91194.00 as on 31.03.2015. Though the balance outstanding advance against the concern officials as on
31.03.2015 has been shown in the cash book, the details of payments and the period to which the outstanding amount relates has not been reflected. Further, it is
noticed that, all the advances have been paid in the form of cash to the officials towards incurring day-to-day contingent expenditure. The advance has been paid
without adjusting the previous one paid to them.
Objection memo issued in this context and in response to the audit objection memo The Local Authority replied that “The advances shown outstanding against
different officer have been adjusted subsequently during the financial year 2016-17 remaining outstanding amount of Rs20995.00 has been recovered from pay bill
of Sri N.R. Mishra, AE. The maintenance of account is being followed as per OPWD code as well as ODA act. The temporary advances are being issued to field
officer (SDO) to meet the emergent and unforeseen expenditure at site. The advance is not a final expenditure, but that is remaining in the closing balance of the
division ash book till it is final adjusted on submission of detail voucher from the concern officer. Regarding contingency there is provision of 1% in work estimates
out of which the general contingent expenditure are being met. Subsidiary cash book are not being maintained here as the pay and allowances are being drawn by
main office.”
But the local authority has not produced any supported document in support of the adjustment. Hence the entire advance of Rs91194.00 held under objection till the
production of adjustment voucher to the next audit. The details of advance position are mentioned in the table -1,2 and 3 below.

As per As per Finance Deptt Notification No-2221/F dtd08.03.2002 and Letter No-15179/DLFA dtd 28.09.2013, advance outstanding for more than one year need to
be surcharged from the officers who granted the same and the payee in equal proportion. Hence total outstanding advance of Rs46,47,738.00 is kept under
objection out of which Rs2403814.00 is suggested for recovery, which is related to the year 2013-14.
Important irregularities noticed in respect of advance position are detailed below
1.The advance ledger of Main Finance was not properly maintained i.e. the vouchers no/date, purpose of advance, payment, name of the sanctioning authority,
adjustment of advance has not been reflected in the ledger.
2. Advance ledger has not been maintained in all the divisions
3.In no case, the purpose of advance has been quoted in the cash book
4. As per the provision of SR-509 Odisha Treasury code (Vol-I), a second advances will not be granted unless the first advance is adjusted.
5. In most cases, the advance payment has been booked as final expenditure without showing it in the closing balance of the cash book.
6. Voucher no has neither been assigned nor quoted in the cash book against each and every advance payment
7. Similarly at the time of adjustment, the voucher no/dtate of the respective advance payment has not been quoted.
8. No advance ledger and outstanding advance register is maintained.

       On checking of the Demand, Collection and Balance of Market Rents w.r.t Connected records for the year 2014-15 it was noticed that a

                        As such an amount of Rs.8,00,93,758.32 is outstanding towards Market Rents as on 31-03-2015. The reason for such non
collection was asked through audit objection memo. In response to the audit objection memo the local authority replied that &quot; The rent for
Ashoka Market and Surya Kiran Market complex was fixed with effect from 01.01.2009 by the cost assessment committee. The rates were
enhanced with effect from 01.01.2009. It was further approved by the Hon'ble Chairman that there will be annual increase @ 10% of the new fee
fixed. The fact of enhancement of rent of shops was communicated to individual licensee of the shops. But the fact of annual increase was not
intimated. In 132th authority meeting held on 20.02.2016, it was approved that the rent may be collected at the enahnced rate w..ef. 01.01.2009
and increment of license fee of 10% may be affected every 3 years w.e.f 01.01.2009 as per previous practice. Accordingly the demand notice was
issued to the all licensees of Ashoka Market and Surya Kiran Market. Some of licensee have deposited their outstanding rent after receipt of
notice. In the mean time decision was taken on Ashoka Market complex regarding unsafe condition and it is decided to be demolished. In Alok
Bharati Complex one office space in 6th floor allotted to CBSE on rented basis. The CBSE was paying @Rs1,18,358.00 without enhanced rate as
per agreement. Now the CBSE is depositing @Rs1,82,217.00 per month. The demand notice is being issued  to CBSE from time to time to deposit
the monthly rent at the enhanced rate. Step have been taken to collect the arrears.&quot; 
 37 / 124

[page 38]
 AUDIT REPORT 
27-06-2017
Hence steps need be taken for collection of the arrear dues and compliance reported to audit. Till then the outstanding dues of  Rs80093758.32 is
held under objection and the amount due for collection for the year 2014-15 i.e,Rs,15505798.50 is suggested for recovery from the  person
responsible for the non collection of the rent.

The reply of local authority is furnished below.
Regarding collection of energy charges of Ashoka Market and Surya Kiran Market ,it is to state that  after receipt of  CESU bill, commerce
Sub-division  immediately served bill to the consumers including arrear. The consumers are also deposited their present demand energy charges
+arrear dues. As the arrear of energy charges a long period of pending,so the pending arrear is gradually reducing every month. In Ashoka Market
& Surya kiran market the collection of energy charges is held up due to imposition of a stay by the Hon'ble High Court Odisha,vide OJC no-1799 of
2000.
Some consumers are deposited energy charges through net banking. As the challans are coming through Finance Deptt. of the BDA, so there is a
delay in receipt of challan from the Finance wing and thus these are not reflected in the monthly ledger.
Moreover BDA is being taken many initiatives by issue of disconnection notice to the individual defaulted consumers for collection of  the arrear
dues from the consumers of different Market complexes(Priyadarsini Market).
Steps may taken to collect the outstanding due at an early date till then the amount of Rs 89,03,430.00 is kept under objection.

14.7 - Irregular payment towards LIC super annuation Scheme  osp  215-216
While checking the payment of LIC Superannuation Scheme w.r.t. paid vouchers, pay acquaintance roll & file no.F & A-I-55/2013, total amount
of Rs.3,85,23,508.00 has been paid to the Chairman of the Trustee, BDA towards employees Superannuation Scheme during the year
2014-15. The above amount is inclusive of employee’s share, which amount deducted from the salary bill of the employees and employer’s
share,  details of which are given below. The employer’s share is equal to the amount deducted from the staff salary towards LIC
Superannuation Scheme. It is further noticed that the employees of BDA are also availing EPF facility for which Rs 1,19,98,856.00  has been
deducted from employee’s salary and the same amount i.e. Rs1,19,98,856.00 deposited from employer’s contribution and Rs11,04,893.00
deposited towards administration charges for EPF during 2014-15. So it was noticed that the employees of BDA are availing EPF facility as well
as Superannuation Scheme facility for which the BDA has to pay much amount for both the scheme.
Hence under the section of 124 of ODA Act, the approval of the State Govt  in this regard is mandatory. There is no such rules in Govt. that
EPF and LIC superannuation scheme will go together as it gives double pensionary benifit to employees.As a result the institution is facing a
loss of Rs.13103749/- per year. Audit objection memo in this regard issued to the local authority. But no reply received till the end of audit.
Hence the amount  spent in this  head is irregular and excess advantages have been given to the employees. It is   suggested for recovery from
the payees failing which the amount may be recovered from the persons responsible for such payment.

14.13 - Loss due to irregular Purchase of Goods OSP-114-119
During  scrutiny of paid vouchers  w.r.t. cash book and other related files it was seen that different types of plants have been purchased for the
parks of BDA amounting to Rs.460150/-.and for purchase of  Garden soil & cow dung manure  Rs.533250/- has also been shown as
expenditure . Besides horticultural equipments  seasonal seedlings were also purchased.
1.      All  the parks were maintained centrally by the Horticulture division  of BDA.  For this purchase   guideline for procurement of goods ( Lt
no. 4939/F/13.2.12) has not been followed. Indent had not been prepared before procurement. The plants were purchased from different
nurseries by short tender call notices (where only two parties haveparticipated). Instead of cancelling and retendering , the tender was accepted
. Sufficient and suitable reason for splitting up of demands were not specified. Due to lack of open tender   in the procurement process  govt.
nurseries /registered nurseries like RPRC,Govt. nursery,Central Hort.nursery,Siraj Nursery & Good Earth etc. could not participated in the
tender process.  Whether rate contract has been matched with that of the Govt. nurseries if prepared may be shown to audit.
As a result  a fair procurement process  was not followed  and  plants were purchased at much more higher rate  as shown in the table
below.The list of plants purchased are given below with a comparative statement of rates with that of the RPRC  ,BBSR.
·         Again there is a own nursery of BDA at Laxmisagar i.e, Laxmisagar nursery  where  golden duranta,broad leaf grass and croton plants
and seasonal seedlings can be grown as per requirement so that the aim and objective of making a own nursery can be fulfilled, otherwise the
the staff employed  and the material used for  nurseyt gains no value.
·         If all the above cited reasons had been taken into consideration the institution might not loss such a good amount   as shown in the last
column of the table which may please  be clarified to audit.
·         After planting , the survival position whether supervised from time to time may be shown to audit.

In response to audit objection memo the Local Authority replied that Horticulture division BDA is maintains large number of parks/sites in and
around Bhubaneswar for greater benefit of all age groups of people. Plantation of seedling of plants is time bound work.  Besides Horticulture
sapling to different Govt and Private organization/ Schools and Colleges on free cost in every year on BDA day to meet all above needs .
Horticulture Nursery Laxmisagar can not produce huge quantity  of sapling within a short time. Due to such type of urgency this division has invited
short tender call notice to supply of sapling. All the short tender call notice was affixed in the noticed board with copies to different department for
wide publication before floating of STC notice due codal procedure was follow and approval of competent authority was obtained as per para 3.
5.24 of OPWD vol-I read with works deptt circular bearing memo no 1223/WF dt20.01.2016 work can be splitted up on suitable parts due to
urgency and spilt up of cases LFA. The supply of sapling material etc well splitted up for speedy and timely timely supply of sapling for the
aforesaid purpose and according  tender/quotation were invited as per necessity for time to time on accordance with codal procedure.
Hence no irregularity has been committed un invitation of short tender observes codal process. The procurements was effected through invitation
of tenders observing codal procedure and lowest tender/quotation has been accepted in each case as such there is no irregularity. Moreover there
is no govt circular or order to frame the estimate basing on rate of RPRC where all required sapling are not always available for supply  in bulky
quantity required by project. So under the circumstances the sapling of ornamental plants have been purchased observing codal procedure on
most transparent and fair manner. Hence there is no loss to BDA and as such this objection may be dropped.
2. Regarding procurement of Planting material the committee constituted on 01.10.2010, comprising EE, Div-I,II,III,IV and Chief Horticulturist and
Chirf Engineer –cum-EM, BDA for finalization of garden soil for use at park and site and nursery. The rate of garden soil approved @Rs720.00/Per
tractor load and cow dung manure @Rs2000.00 per tractor load with transportation. Void and royalty charges as per prevailed rate. Accordingly
these items have been procure through tender. The lowest rates are much less than the approved rate. Hence the para may be drooped.
2. The reply of local authority is accepted and  objection 2 relating to table 2 is dropped.
1.The reply of Local Authority is not convincing and not to the point as raised by audit. During the procurement of goods no fair procedure has
been maintained. Due to short tender call notices,wide circulation of tender hampered and all goods are purchased from Swastik Nursery. During
the course of audit a comparative statement has been prepared by audit in table -1 which shows how much excess amount was paid to Swastik
Nursery in comparision to rate of RPRC BBSR(rate available on net ). If aschedule of rate of plants obtained from Govt.Nurseries or Registered nurseries  of BBSR purchase can be done in reasonable rate .Due to lack of open tender the institution fa ce s a loss of Rs.379350/- which is suggested for
recovery.

14.14 - Irrergular expenditure towards renovation of Netaji Subash park  osp119-120
While going through  file no. OS-417-Hort./2013 ,it was seen that Renovation of Netaji Subash Park was done during 2013-14 where following
development had been done and the following information were found.
1.       Total area of the park-170000 sqft
2.       Garden area to be developed – 60000 sqft
3.      Tile garden path -500 rft
4.      Lawn area-15000 sqft
For the above work  garden soil of Rs.174000/-,cow dung of Rs.10000/-,Broad leaf grass 5000 sqft ,golden duranta of 9600 nos and other
ornamental plants of 428 nos  are planted.
But after the cyclonic storm Philine  ,it was noted down at page no. 13/N of the said file that  after massive devastation ,renovation work is
needed  at the park. But what were damaged nowhere mentioned in the file.
But an estimate amounting Rs.494600/- was prepared for the following work as on 9.5.14
1. Garden soil - Rs.323000/-
2. Cow dung - Rs.30000/-
3. Broad leaf grass -4000 sqft @ Rs.12/sqft.
4. Golden duranta - 4800 nos
5. Other ornamental plants – 260 nos  .
During 2013-14 broad leaf grass was planted in the lawn area during June 2013. And a good no. of ornamental plantation was made in the
park. So in the cyclone the soil must be intact as it was already covered by broad leaf grass  and a good no. of plants.
In response to audit objection memo the local authority replied that the park suffered from severe damage in phailine in Oct,2013 and as such
needed renovation for which estimate for Rs4,94,600.00 was sanctioned after administrative approval of CE-cum-EM , BDA. Thus estimate was
sanction basing on the damage assist by the field officer. After physical visit of the park. Since the estimate have been framed on realistic basis
as per requirement at site the apprehension of Audit is unfounded. Hence the objection may be dropped.
Basing on the files registers and the records supplied by the division the objection was raised . The file lacks detailed field survey report by the
higher authority. Renovation of Netaji Subhash Park already completed during 2013-14 where broad leaf grass  planted in all the lawn area.

head amounting Rs.371000(Rs.323000+48000) is kept under objection till satisfactory compliance.

14.17 - Excess payment of Service tax    osp 258
 61 / 124

[page 62]
 AUDIT REPORT

1. During scrutiny of paid vouchers w.r.t. bank book and other connected files  it come to notice that a sum of Rs  5420800/- was incurred
expenditure  for the period from  1.1.14 to 30.11.14 for professional fees and paid to M/S JLL for consultancy service . Out of the total amount
of Rs. 5420800/- , Rs.580800/- was paid towards service tax. But as per circular no.30/2012 service tax dt.20.6.12 Ministry of Finance
(Rev.Deptt.) Govt. of India, both service provider and service recipient are liable to pay tax  50%  each. Hence out of total 580800/- a sum of 
Rs.290400/-was paid in excess which is suggested for recovery.

for payment vide invoice no BDA/2014-15/001 dt 12.06.2014.

Out of the total bill amount 80 percent was paid vide voucher no-82/19.07.2014 of Rs12,26,971.00 and balance 20 percent to be paid after
successful completion of the project.
In this bill service tax 12 percent amounting to Rs171429.00 was paid to the agency towards service tax. But as per service tax letter no 30/2012 dt
20.06.2012 Sl no 09 minister of finance (Rev deptt) Govt of India, both service provider and service recipient is liable to pay tax at 50 percentage.
So 50 percent of Rs171429.00 paid in excess may be recovered from the agency.
Similarly in Voucher no 135/24.11.2014 amount paid to Pyramid consultancy pvt ltd towards digitalization of work against invoice no 2014-15/003
Bill amount                         Rs758169.00
Service tax                          Rs 90980.00
 
As per the above circular of GOI 50 percent to be paid by both the service provider and recipient. Hence 50 percent tax comes to Rs.Rs45490.00.
 As a result  a total sum of Rs335890/-(290400+45490) paid in excess towards service tax deposit.
Audit objection memo in this regard issued to the local authority. But, no reply received till the end of audit.
Steps may be taken which is to recover these amount from the Consultancies failing which the following persons are held responsible.
During exit conference the local authority furnished the following reply.
                                    1.  Earlier on dtd. 31.03.2015 vide letter No. 145/226, National Director, JLL had complied the same type of audit objection
that the notification letter (30/2012 Service Tax) point No.9 applies to works contract services when the said services are being provided by a
non-corporate entity to a Business entity. M/s JLL a global consulting firm was ed through a bidding process for setting up PMU unit in BDA with a
 62 / 124

[page 63]
 AUDIT REPORT 
27-06-2017
purpose of providing capacity building services, procurement advisory, policy advisory and transaction advisory for PPP projects (copy of the
above said letter is enclosed). The condition of JLL are being considered after taking approval of the committee members and the Authority of
BDA.  The photocopy of  the proceedings of the meeting are enclosed for taking further action
   2. As per Notification No. 30/2012, Reverse charge mechanism is applicable only when the service provider is an individual, partnership- not a
body corporate-and the service receiver is a body corporate like the Pvt. Limited company, here the entire tax has to be paid by the provider and
the receiver can avail cenvat  for same as well. Since, Services were being provided by a Pvt. Ltd Company (i.e. M/s Pyramid  IT consulting Pvt.
Ltd.), therefore, the service provider will be liable to charge and pay the tax amount. In this scenario, service recipient (BDA) would not be liable to
pay the tax under reverse charge mechanism.  Letter has been issued vide letter No. 9724/BDA dtd. 24.04.2017 to M/s Pyramid IT Consulting Pvt.
Ltd. to refund the excess payment of service tax of Rs131235/- .
The amount derived by audit in both cases is a loss to the institution due to excess payment of service tax. So steps may be taken which is to
recover these amount from the Consultancies failing which the following persons are held responsible for the loss.

14.18 - Loss due to unconditional acceptance of demands of JLL OSP-259
       During scrutiny of  file no. MIS-03/10 vol-II/2011  regarding project  monitoring Unit  (PMU) relating to JLL , it come to notice that  the
agreement  was renewed (JLL for project management ,finance and  urban planning) on 15.1.2014 where  the  following points  were
mentioned .
Fees : BDA shall pay JLL fee of INR 440000/- plus taxes as applicable(from 1.1.15-31.12.15) & 10% hike from the next year i.e for next 12
months.
But amendment of agreement was done on 14.11.14 where both parties entered into agreement effective from 1.1.15. (1)Here the agency
demanded to engage 2 more staff to carry out work of BMC and PPP mode . But  BMC have  its own CLTC and Project Officer to carry out
different projects. (2)Success fee sharing was increased to 5%  from 0.25% and (3)retainer fee increased to 3.00 lakhs  from 0.5%. Again  the
agency was claiming for extra charges for ppp mode projects and other extra work . There are other consultancy services assigned in different
divisions and for new projects. So engagement of extra man power by JLL and acceptance of all conditions in 1,2,3 without any negotiation
seems to be considered in the better self of the agency than the local institution. The reason of giving so much favours to agency may be
clarified and compliance reported to audit.
Audit objection memo in this regard issued to the local authority but no reply received till the end of audit. Hence Rs,150949/- paid in excess
towards engagement of one more man power is kept under objection.

In response to the audit objection memo the local Authority replied that &quot;BDA is a public sector under taking of the Govt of Odisha and its
function with a motive to provide services to public for improvement and creation of infrastructure of the capital city. So frequent meeting as per
needs of the BDA as well as for infrastructure development of capital city are held at regular intervals for which distinguish members/high
dignitaries of govt are invited to participate in the deliberation of the meetings. So like other public limited company and public undertaking
necessary provision for providing refreshment to the participant have been made in the estimate. And the maximum monetary limit of Rs15000.00
per month have been sanction by the competent authority for the above purpose. The copy of which enclosed and as such no irregularity is
involved in it. Moreover all the rules and regulation applicable to Govt Deptt are not applicable automatically to BDA since it is a public service
oriented under taking entity having its own norms and regulations. Hence the limits of Rs15000.00 per month fixed by competent authority towards
refreshment is justified and the monthly expenditure on refreshment has never been exceeded the approved maximum limit in any month. Hence
the objection may be dropped in view of the reply.
The reply of the Local Authority is not at all convincing. Further the office order bearing no-2570/08.02.2016 regarding refreshment expenditure is
not related to the period under audit i.e. 2014-15. Hence the expenditure incurred  contradicts the Govt order and cannot be admitted in audit.
Hence amount of Rs.135563.00 is kept under objection till regularisation of the same.

tax in a  favour of Top Guarding Security Services and Rs.161375/-was paid to Oriental Security Services towards service.  So as per circular
of Govt of India, Ministry of Finance and Revenue Deptt, notification no 30/2012 service tax point 8 says in respect of service provided or
agreed to be provided by way of supply of manpower for any purpose in 25 percent and 75 percent ratio. So 25 percent of total service tax paid
 in excess i.e,Rs.147764/- to Top guarding Security Services  and Rs.40344/-to Oriental Security Services is suggested for recovery from the
person concerned.

i.                   All purchase of new vehicles and equipment except those for  which concurrence of Finance Department is available for
the year 2000-2001 shall be stopped forthwith
ii.                  There shall be a ban on purchase of luxury vehicle. Luxury vehicle means the vehicle other than non AC diesel
Ambassador cars or non AC diesel Jeeps. The purchase of any petrol 4 wheel vehicle is banned henceforth.

15.2 - Splitting up of work of Div-I OSP-122-130
Sub- Splitting of work (Division-I)
            As per clause 3.5.24 of the Odisha Public Works Department (O.P.W.D) Code, the splitting up of the work into several parts at the time
of calling of tenders is a measure which should be justified by circumstances and must be in the interest of the work. It must not be resorted to
with a view to evade the operation of any prescribed limit. Where ever any work is executed by splitting up, the abstract of sanctioned estimate
for the work should also be split up & approved. The split up portion of the work can be treated as a separate work so far as acceptance of
tender is concerned.  Clause 3.5.25 of P.W.D Code provides that when a work covered by one notice of tender is proposed to be divided
amongst a number of contractors, a clause to that effect should be ed in the notice inviting tenders as well as in the tender form .Care should
be taken to see that portion of work allotted to different contractors are of distinct nature and are sufficiently separated to make interference with
supplies of materials and labour impossible.
           Further, as per Works Department Circular No. 20796 dated 24.10.2005 on “ Ban on splitting-up of work- 2006”, Government of Odisha
have made some provisions for splitting-up of work as follows-
i)- Under non-plan scheme such as repair to road C.D. works, buildings and other irrigation & P.H. limiting to Rs. 5.00 lakhs may be split up into
 70 / 124

[page 71]
 AUDIT REPORT 
27-06-2017
small reaches after obtaining approval of competent authority.
ii)- In extraordinary situation of emergent nature such as breach of roads, repair of C.D. works, restoration of structures damaged due to natural
calamities and also in case of visit of V.I.Ps and V.V.I.Ps. split-up of work may be done without any financial limit on approval of competent
authority.
          On checking & scrutiny of the works case records with reference to concern MBs & other relevant records & registers of Division-I, it is
found that some of the works/projects, as detailed below, have been executed by splitting up the entire works/project cost to an amount below
Rs. 50000.00. Further, in most cases, the splitted works were assigned to the same contractor for execution. Audit was asked through objection
memo to clarify  to audit, under which circumstances the works were splitted up and assigned to the same contractor for execution.

In response to the audit objection memo the Local Authority replied that As per Para 3-5-24 of OPWD code, a work can be splitted up in to suitable
parts/reaches for facility if smooth speedily execution and completion of work execution and completion of work. Moreover works department Govt
 73 / 124

[page 74]
 AUDIT REPORT 
27-06-2017
of Odisha, circular bearing no 1223/W dt 20.1.2006 stipulated that works limiting to Rs5 lakhs under non plan scheme can also also be splitted in
to small reaches on approval of competent authority. In addition to that works of urgent nature and works necessitated on account of visit of VVIPs
may be splitted up into reaches/parts for expeditious execution without any financial limit. The works pointed out by audit were of urgent in nature
and have been splitted up into suitable reaches for facility of speedy completion within the target date set by the authority. Secondly work at list
no-1 of the list was of extremely urgent in nature due to inauguration of the same by VVIPs and as such the same work was spliited into different
reaches for speedy execution for completion within date. In each and every case, approval of competent authority (Executive Engineer) has been
obtained as per codal rule and Govt. circulars in force as Executive Engineer is competent to accord technical sanction of estimates of works up to
50Lakhs. Secondly total value of split up portion of works comes within Rs5 lakhs as laid down in the Govt. circular referred above. Since in each
and every case necessary approval has been obtained to split the works for facility of speedy execution and completion of work in accordance with
the guidelines laid down in para 3-5-24 of OPWD code Vol-I read with Memo No 1223 dt 20.01.2006 of circular of works department, no irregularity
is involved in it.
In each and every case, competitive tenders have been floated for split up of the work in most transparent and fair manner observing codal
procedure and the tender of the lowest bidder has been accepter without any deviation from the codal provisions.
Regarding the observations of audit on award of works to same agency in some of the cases, this is to state that tenders were invited through
tender call notice in most fair and transparent manner which were widely circulated as per codal procedures and the work has been awarded to the
lowest bidder only in each case. If in some cases, the lowest tenderers happened to be contractor, it is a matter of sheer coincident rather than
intentional since tenderers quoting the lowest rate has been ed for award of work in each case and as such this division has nothing to do with it.
In view of the facts stated above, it is clear that the list of works as pointed out by local fund audit has been executed on split up basis in
accordance with the procedure laid down in para-3-5-24 of OPWD code vol-I read with the works department, Govt of Odisha Memo No 1223/dt
20.01.2006 and as such no irregularity is committed in the aforesaid cases. Hence the objection raised in the POM issued vide Memo
No-19/17.11.2016 may please be dropped.
The reply furnished by the Local Authority is not to the point of objection raised
1.       In some cases after approval of the original estimate, suggestion has been given to the split up  work on the ground of urgency. But on the
circumstances that gave rise to the urgency situation has not been clarified/reflected.
2.       In most cases, the splitted up works in respect of a single estimate has been executed by the same contractor which is not at all a time
saving process. As such the very purpose of splitting up of work is not at all beneficial. At the same time it is contradictory to the word “urgency”
which has been used in the file for splitting up of work
Hence the local authority is advised to execute the works as per codal provision of O.P.W.D. 
 
15.3 - Splitting up of work of Div-IV OSP-177-190
                 As per clause 3.5.24 of the Odisha Public Works Department (O.P.W.D) Code, the splitting up of the work into several parts at the
time of calling of tenders is a measure which should be justified by circumstances and must be in the interest of the work. It must not be
resorted to with a view to evade the operation of any prescribed limit. Where ever any work is executed by splitting up, the abstract of
sanctioned estimate for the work should also be split up & approved. The split up portion of the work can be treated as a separate work so far
as acceptance of tender is concerned.  Clause 3.5.25 of P.W.D Code provides that when a work covered by one notice of tender is proposed to
be divided amongst a number of contractors, a clause to that effect should be ed in the notice inviting tenders as well as in the tender form
.Care should be taken to see that portion of work allotted to different contractors are of distinct nature and are sufficiently separated to make
interference with supplies of materials and labour utilised.
Further, as per Works Department Circular No . 20796 dated 24.10.2005  on “ Ban on splitting-up of work- 2006 ”, Government of Odisha
have made some provisions for splitting-up of work as follows-
i)- Under non-plan scheme such as repair to road C.D. works, buildings and other irrigation & P.H. limiting to Rs. 5.00 lakhs may be
split up into small reaches after obtaining approval of competent authority.
ii)- In extraordinary situation of emergent nature such as breach of roads, repair of C.D. works, restoration of structures damaged
due to natural calamities and also in case of visit of V.I.Ps and V.V.I.Ps. split-up of work may be done without any financial limit on
approval of competent authority.
 
                      On checking & scrutiny of the works case records with reference to concern MBs & other relevant records & registers of
Division-IV, it is found that some of the works/projects, as detailed below, have been executed by splitting up the entire works/project cost to an
amount below Rs. 50000.00. Further, in most cases, the splitted works were assigned to the same contractor for execution.The local authority

In response to the audit objection memo the Local Authority replied that Division-IV of BDA deals with the execution of original works both internal
and external as well as looks after the repair and maintenance of both internal and external E.I. works of mrket complex, BDA staff quarter, repair
of steet light under BDA. Sometimes, urgent repair works are undertaken for repair and restoration of defective electrical installation both internal
and external on urgent basis. For speedy and timely execution of works, the electrical works have been splitted into different reached for speedy
and smooth execution of electrical repairs works in accordance with the para 3.5.24 clause  of OPWD code vil-I. Moreover , works department,
Govt of Odisha memo no 1223 dtd 20.01.2006 also pemits that the works under non plan scheme can also be splitted into small reaches after
obtaining approval of competent authority.
                Hence urgent works of repair in nature have undertaken by this division after approval of competent authority in each and every case.
The list of works as pointed out by the Local Fund Audit were of urgent in nature and the same has been splitted up for timely and speedy
completion of the same on approval of split up estimate by the competent authority.
                In view of the facts stated above, it is clear that list of works as pointed out by the LFA have been executed in accordance with the Para
3.5.24 clause of OPWD code Vol-I read with work deptt circular No 1223 dt 20.01.2006. AS such there is no violation of codal provision.
                Regarding objection of LFA on award of works to the same agency in most of the cases, this is to state that tenders have been invited
through issues of tender call notice in each case which were widely  circulated among the local official as well as in the notice board as per the
guidelines laid down in the codal provisions for wide publicity and the work has benn awarded to the lowest tenderer in each case. If in some of te
cases, the lowest tenderer happen to be the same contractor,it is a matter of coincident rather than intentional since the renderer qualify the lowest
rate has been ed for award of the work in each and every cases and as such this division has nothing to do with it.Hence the objection  of LFA may
please be dropped in view of the aforesaid reply.
                 The reply furnished by the Local Authority is not to the point of objection raised
1.       In some cases after approval of the original estimate, suggestion has been given to the split up  work on the ground of urgency. But on the
circumstances that gave rise to the urgency situation has not been clarified/reflected.
 100 / 124

[page 101]
 AUDIT REPORT 
27-06-2017
2.       In most cases, the splitted up works in respect of a single estimate has been executed by the same contractor which is not at all a time
saving process. As such the very purpose of splitting up of work is not at all beneficial. At the same time it is contradictory to the word      urgency  
   which has been used in the file for splitting up of work
Hence the local authority is advised to execute the works as per codal provision of O.P.W.D. 
           .

Nagar Zone K-III B drawing no.B/1391” it come to notice that before construction of work field visit was conducted by Spl.Secy. to Govt.
G.A.Deptt.,Director of Estate CE cum EM ,BDA & EE  Div.II BDA. After site visit  report estimate for the work has been prepared . In the

the authority .
 102 / 124

[page 103]
 AUDIT REPORT 
27-06-2017
The reply furnished by the local authority is furnished below.
The total area is divided into three patches. To start the work in the first instance cleaning estimate was approved for 1 patch only which was
executed during March 2014 as GA department was pressing hard for early demarcation and handling over of the plots to the respective
allottees. The tender for construction of road was finalized/accepted and the date of commencement of the work was 16.07.2014. During this
gap of four months shrubs and some thorny jungles development over the entire patch and other two patches also due to the effect of
monsoon. During execution, there was necessary of cleaning the other patches (area of 41,169.32 Sqmtr) for which approval was given by
CE-cum-EM during his site visit and in the site order book. The same was also approved in the deviation statement.
The objection hence dropped .

          On scrutiny of the case record with reference to the concern MB & other relevant records, it is revealed that measurement of the work

& total executed amount comes to Rs. 159149.00 vide page no.-194.
A)-      As such, Rs.1114.00(Rs.159149.00 – Rs.158035.00) has been paid in excess than the agreement value. The original deviation

B)-Excess rate allowed
Further , on scrutiny of the same, it is revealed that a sum of Rs.49600.00 has been paid vide item no-03 at page-70-71 of the MB towards
supplying,fitting, fixing well seasoned sal wood chaukatha of size (0’5”X0’2 ½”) of approved quality including all etc. @ Rs.77500/Cum for
 106 / 124

 AUDIT REPORT 
27-06-2017
0.64Cum. The rate for the same item as per estimate is Rs.70663.00.
   But the rate admissible as per SR-2013 & Analysis of rate is calculated below-
Rate for 0.028Cum
Salwood Scantling sawn 0.028Cum Rs.32537.00 911.04
Carpenter Special 0.5no Rs.205.00 102.5
Man Mulia 0.5no 150 75
OC   7.50% 81.64

As such, the rate admissible for the same item is Rs.46934.00(i.e Rs.44708+ 4.98% as per agreement)/Cum
Amount permissible for 0.64Cum is Rs. 30038.00(i.e Rs.46934.00 X 0.64)
Excess amount paid – Rs.19562.00(i.e Rs.49600.00 – Rs.30038.00)
Hence, it may be clarified why the amount of Rs.19562.00 will not be suggested for recovery.
C)-Utilisation/Disposal of old materials
    On verification of the case record, it is noticed that a sum of Rs.5917.90 has been paid vide item no.-01 at page-69 of the MB towards
dismantling of doors & windows including removal of frame, hinges etc. complete @ Rs.155.00/sq.mt. for 38.18sq.mt. Further, it is found that
Rs.1554.45 has been paid vide item no.-2(a) at page-70 of the MB towards labour for fixing of re-use shutter @ Rs.215/sq.mt for 7.23sq.mt. But
the utilization/disposal of the balance quantity of dismantled materials i.e 30.95sq.mt (38.18 – 7.23) has not been mentioned in the record.
     Hence, the balance unutilized old materials of 30.95sq.mt @ Rs.155/-sqm which comes to Rs.4797/-is suggested for recovery.
In response to the audit objection memo the local authority replied that
Voucher No-5/03.07.2014
(A)The total value of works executed works out to Rs159149.00 against the agreement value Rs158035.00 and the deviation statement has
already been sanctioned by competent authority in accordance with the para 6-3-17 of OPWD code Vol-I and as such there is no irregularity
involved in payment of bill. Copy of the sanction deviation statement is enclosed herewith for reference of the LFA. Hence the objection may be
dropped.
(B)Excess allowed
As per guideline in the schedule of rate, the rates of goods item are equivalent to the rates of Orissa Forest Development Corporation (OFDC) on
the average. If any change to the rates are made by the OFDC, revised rates shall be taken into consideration while arriving at the finished rates.
Moreover it has also been clarified in the S/R that for wood work, the supply rates as fixed by OFDC shall prevailed over the rates provided in the
scheduled rate. In this connection an exact of relevant provision of book of schedule of rate is enclosed here with. In the present case pointed out
by LFA the estimate of the wood item has been framed basing on the basic OFDC rate of Rs55107.00/cum as provided in the sanction estimate
and the copy of the cash memo of OFDC is enclosed herewith in support of the basic rate of wood item of OFDC as annexure –II. The finished
item of wood work has been arrived at Rs70663.00 basing on the basic rate of wood @Rs55107.00 as provide in the estimate. In this connection,
an analysis of rate is enclosed herewith (Annex-III) which substantiates the calculation of finished wood item at Rs70603.00. Hence there is no
question of excess rate provided in the sanctioned estimate for these rates.
Secondly competitive item rate tender was invited for this work in question for ion of agency to execute the work. 2 nos of tenderer participated in
the tender and tender of Sri Sarbajit Mahal was the 1st lowest tender wherein he has quoted 14.67 percentage excess over the estimated cost put
to the tender. So he was called for negotiation to reduce his rates and accordingly he has reduced his quoted in some of the items and his
negotiated tender value worked out to 4.98 percent excess over the estimated cost and as such his tender was accepted. In this connection this is
to pin point here that the first lowest tender has quoted Rs91,000.00 /cum for this wood item but subsequently on being called for negotiation, he
reduce his rate to Rs77500.00/cum and his tender was accepted at 4.98 percent excess on the whole over the estimated cost.  The negotiated rate
for this item @Rs77500.00 was incorporated in the agreement basing on which payment for the work done has been made. So no irregularity has
 107 / 124

[page 108]
 AUDIT REPORT 
27-06-2017
been committed on execution and payment for this item.
In view of the facts stated above it is amply clear   that analysis of this wood item has been made on the basis of OGDC rate which prevails over
the analysis of rate made by LFA based on S/R. More over the rate at which payment for this item has been made is negotiated rate of the first
lowest tenderer which has been by the competent authority as per provision of OPDW code. Hence no excess payment has been paid to the
agency over and above the agreement rate which was drawn basing on the lowest negotiated tender of the agency. Therefore, the question of
excess payment does not arise and as such this  objection may be dropped in view of the reply.
(C )Utilisation/Disposal of old materials
                Out of 38.18 sq mtr dismantled wood, 7.23 sq mtr dismantled wood was found to be in usable condition and as such this quantity of
wood have been utilized vide item no-2 (B) of the bill and balance dismantled wood was found damaged in pieces which were of no use in any
work.
                In this connection necessary has been furnished by the Asst Engineer in charge of the work vide page 68-69 of MB 1378. Hence this
objection may be dropped in view of the reply. 
(A) But the original deviation statement of the concerned work was made available to audit.
(B) The rates of OFDC on the average has not been quoted or justified in the reply. Further the copy of cash memo of OFDC produced has been
issued to AVR Infratech Pvt Ltd where as Sri Sarabjit Mahala is the executant of this work. 
Copy of the analysis of rates attached to the reply also does not match with the item as depicted in the concerned estimate as well MB.
(C ) Report regarding disposal of balance dismantled wood has not been made available. 
Basing on the above facts the reply of the Local Authority is not to the point. Hence Rs24359.00(19562+4797) suggested for recovery from the
person responsible.

PARA: 16 AUDIT ON UNITS / DEPARTMENT 
PARA: 17 AUDIT ON SCHEMES / PROGRAMMES
17.1 - Scheme
PARA: 18 MISCELLANEOUS
18.1 - UTILISATION OF Compounding Fees Planning Section OSP-271
As per Clause -3 (V) of Odisha Notification No-2534/dtd31.12.2007 and No 2390/31.12.2008 at least 50 percent of coumpounding fees
collected shall be utilised for development purpose by the Development Authority. In case of coumpounding fees collected towards
regularisation of parking deficit area in respect of commecrcial/mixed land use building the fund so collected by the authority shall be utilised for
development of parking facilities.
On checking of bank book and General ledger it was noticed that an amount of Rs1,28,89,180.00 has been collected towards compounding
fees during the year 2014-15. Hence, as per aforesaid guideline at least 50 percent of the above collection i.e. Rs64,44,590.00 should be
utilised towards development purpose during the year 2014-15.
            The local authority asked to produce the details of projects if any taken by the authority out of compounding fees with budgetary
provision, actual amount spent, connected files/records, MBs etc., but no reply furnished by the local authority till completion of audit. Hence the
amount of Rs64,77,590.00 kept under objection till production of records to next audit. 
18.2 - Non Revocation of Bank Guarrantee Planning Section OSP-277-280
On verification of the bank guarantee register w.r.t concerned plan approval files, it was noticed that the validity of the bank guaranties
submitted by the applicants in lieu of security deposit as detailed below for approval of building plan have been expired. Neither the concern
applicants submitted the extended bank guarantee/ completion certificate of the project nor the same have been revoked by the local authority.
As per ODA Act’1982, the BDA should do field verification so as to ascertain whether the applicant has constructed the building as per
approved plan or not. But during checking of connected files in no case the site verification has been done by the Local Authority.
Further , as per clause-7(2) and 77 of the building standard regulation 2008 in case the construction are not as made as per approved plan then
the security deposit should be forfeited and deposited to the BDA account. As the site verification has not been done by the BDA it cannot be
 113 / 124

[page 114]
 AUDIT REPORT 
27-06-2017
ascertained whether building is as per approved plan or not.
In absence of extended bank guarantee and completion certificate of the project, If the said bank guarantees have been revoked and credited
to BDA account, the BDA would have been got interest on the total bank guarantee valueof Rs.90,30,351.00

Hence the reason for such lapses asked through audit objection memo. In response to the audit objection memo the local authority replied that 
As per the observations of the audit, steps have already been initiated to make field verification regarding the completion of projection of project to
ascertain whether the project is completed as per approved building plan within the permissible norms. If deviation is detected in respect of
approved plan of the building, action will be taken to revoke permission as per the procedure laid down in ODA Act,1982 as well as BDA(P and BS)
Regulation 2008 (amended 2013). Moreover no occupancy certificate will be issued in case of deviation without approval by the competent
authority. So there is no loss to BDA in cases of expiry of B.B. as listed in the objection memo. Hence the objection memo dropped.&quot;
      The reply of the Local Authority is not satisfying the points of query. Since the expired bank guarantees have not been revoked and credited to
the BDA account, the institution sustained a loss to the tune equal to the interest on Rs90,30,351.00.
     Further no records regarding the steps taken by the authority to make field verification could be made available to audit. As such in absence of
field verification the positions of construction of building as per approved plan might have not been ascertained and it contradicts ODA act 1982
and clause 7(2) and 77 od building standard Regulation 2008.
    Hence the Local Authority is requested to take necessary and sincere steps towards field verification. Further steps need be taken for invocation
of the expired bank guarantees and shown to next audit. Till then Rs90,30,351.00 is held under objection. 
 
18.3 - IRREGULAR ALLOTMENT OF LAND TO SOA OSP-272-274
 115 / 124

[page 116]
 AUDIT REPORT 
27-06-2017
Ref: File name 1.-Misc-LA-2/99 & Misc-LA-2/99(pt.-1)      
During scrutiny of paid vouchers w.r.t bank book it came to notice that a sum of Rs4,92,85,000.00 was paid to GA deptt  vide voucher
no-130/28.08.2014 for cost of 19.714 acres of land @25lakhs/per acres. Again a sum of Rs9,86,000.00 was paid in favour of sub- Registrar,
Khandagiri for lease deed of the above land in mauza Ghatikia, (K-8, Kalinga Nagar meet from BDA fund).
BDA allotted 19.714 acres of land in favour of SOA university. BDA has received an amount of Rs7,79,89,779.00 from SOA university towards
the cost of land. But GA Deptt is charging Rs11,70,13,542.00 (Premium Rs4,92,85,000.00 + Interest Rs6,77,28,542.00) from BDA for the land
allotted in favour of SOA university. So it is mentioned here at page no 165-C of File No-L-4/97 (Pt-1) of Land Section that BDA has received an
amount of Rs7,79,89,779.00 from SOA university. So that it is not possible to pay an amount of Rs11,70,13,542.00 for that land.
During course of checking the above files it came to notice that GA deptt has allotted in Oct, 1992, 128.70 acres of land to BDA in Ghatikia and
Shampur with the condition that the land so allotted should be used for development of residential plots /houses. Accordingly BDA had
developed a plotted scheme at Kalinga Nagar over the land. However it was noticed that BDA had allotted 19.17 acres of land in KNPDS area
in favour of S O A for engineering college (which is actually a medical college)without any advertisement or competitive bidding/auction, but in a
phased manner during 1999-2005 at a residential rate i.e. @11,44,000.00 per acre. Ac 7.204 acres allotted in an institutional rate. But vide GA
Deptt Letter No-6146/GA dt 21.05.2003 page 75C regarding allotment of 9 acres of land to SOA where the proposal of BDA was rejected GA
deptt  and directed not to deliver any land to SOA but to use social housing project. But the meeting held on 30.05.2003 the 9 acres of land was
allotted to the SOA Institution.
Letter No 210/BDA 13.12.2003 Misc LA -2-at page no 68,69, here it is mentioned that about deliver of 9 acres of land to SOA and again here
also decided to deliver more 19 acres of land as it is ditch area not suitable for housing scheme(whether this land delivered to the
instututioncouldnot be known).
The HT line of 3.84 acres of land was directed to the institution for plantation with proper guidance of BDA, horticulture unit. No construction
work of any nature shall be undertaken over this land.
During course of checking it is seen that 19.714 acre was allotted in favour of Sikshya O Anusandhan  University. Out of which 7.204 acres of
land was allotted with approval of GA deptt. For the rest 12.510 acres no approval has been obtained from GA deptt. Rather GA deptt strictly
warned not to give land for institution purpose but to use only for housing purpose. But the land was allotted to SOA from 30.07.1999 to
18.07.2007 in a phase manner and for which SOA had deposited an amount of Rs7,79,89,779.00. So BDA without making any competitive
bidding or auction handed over a huge size of plot measuring 8.953 acres at K-8, Kalinga Nagar, Ghatikia on instalment basis. Without
imposing interest @15% for late payment.
GA deptt in order no CA/AL (B) 7/97/26663/CA dt 14.11.2012 where deptt allowed to BDA to transfer land measuring 19.714 acres in favour of
SOA out of the total land allotted.BDA shall pay a sum of Rs15,69,88,712.00 only towards balance premium and interest due upto 313.08.2012
before execution of lease deed.
But before getting approval from deptt now building plan was approved by BDA vide its letter no-4819/BDA dt 27.07.2004 and how could NOC
was given to avail loan from UTI bank vide letter no 13520/AL dt 16.09.2005 i.e. before GA deptt over date 14.11.2012.
As revealed from the files cited SOA has to pay land under institutional purpose for 19.714 acres of land @ Rs1,09,68,494.00 per acres
including the HT line area, as construction has been done on this land. As such total valuation of the land comes to  Rs21,62,32,891.00 plus for
the corner plot extra cost amounting to Rs36,25,000.00.Hence total cost comes Rs21,98,57,891.00
Again add Water charges @ 2000.00 for the each 1250 sqft (LIG  Plot) for for 19.714 comes to ((43550X19.714)/1250)X2000= Rs13,73,672.00
has not been recovered from the institution.
Again add registration cost of Rs9,86,300.00 should be deposited by the institution.
Add Interest calculation P/336/C @15% per annum which comes to Rs6,89,64,043.00 as on 03.10.2012. Subtract Rs.77989779/- the institution
had paid.
Hence a sum of Rs.21,31,89,127.00 is suggested for recovery from SOA University.
In response to the audit objection memo the local authority replied that
Govt in GA deptt order no-16195/dt14.10.1992 has allotted land acres128.270 in mouza Ghatikia and shampur in favour of BDA @Rs6.5 lakh
per acres for plotted development scheme. In letter no-9026 dtd 31.07.1994, the rate of premium was reduced to Rs2.00 lakh per acres in
respect of above land.
                BDA developed plotted scheme (K-8 Kalinga Nagar) over an area of 99.459 in mouza Ghatikia. Secondly an area of acres 19.714
was allotted at different stages used for institutional purpose in Ghatikia.
                Govt in GA deptt order no-26663 dtd 14.11.2012 allowed BDA for transfer of acre 19.714 of Land in favour of SOA for institutional
purpose @ Rs25.00lakh per acres with interest. GA deptt vide letter no- 23540 dtd 25.08.2014 intimated BDA for payment of Rs 49285000.00
towards land premium for above acres 19.714 land pending finalization of payment of interest.
                Hon’ble High Court has passed order on 16.07.2014 in WP © no-19143/2013 to execute lease deed in favour of SOA University
 116 / 124

[page 117]
 AUDIT REPORT 
27-06-2017
within eight weeks. BDA paid above amount to GA deptt vide treasury challan no-2 dtd 03.09.2014 towards premium @Rs25.00 lakh per acre.
Further an amount of Rs986300.00 was also paid to Sub-Registrar Khandagiri towards registration feees.
                The matter of payment of Rs77989779.00  by SOA and claim of GA deptt amounting to Rs117013542.00 on BDA was placed in 123rd
authority meeting vide Agenda item no-13 page 165/c of land file no L-4/94 (pt)
                The information regarding allotment of Ac19.714 land in favour of SOA University and payment received by BDA including cost,
interest and development cost is dealt in allotment branch, BDA which will be complied by them.
                The Audit memo relating to Land section may kindly be treated as complied from land section.
The compliance of Allottment section is yet be received. Till receipt of the compliance  the amount of Rs. 21,98,57,891.00 is kept under
objection.
 
18.4 - LOSS DUE TO IRREGULAR ALLOTMENT OF OFFICE BUILDING TO OBC BANK AND ATM COUNTER LAND/ALLOTMENT
SECTION-OSP-282-284
During scrutiny of Bank Book and General Ledger of Main Finance for the year 2014-15, it was noticed that an amount of Rs 1,97,172.00
collected from OBC, BDA towards rent of Bank and ATM for the year 2014-15.
But while going through the file No AL-II-32/94 relating to OBC bank,  the Agreeement copy or lease deed  regarding the allotment of ground
floor of Akash Sova Building  to OBC Bank has not been found. But  in this file it was decided to collect rent @ Rs 6.00 per sq.ft with 10% hike
in every 2 year and imposition of interest  @ 16% per annum on arrear rent (ref-page no. 40 /c).
                Again while going through the same file No AL-II-32/94, it came to notice that from 10.04.1992 bank has been started initially at 872
sq. ft carpet area. From 23.07.1997 bank space was increased by 860 sqft carpet area more, so the total area came to 1732 sq.ft.  Again from
08.02.2001 more 782 sqft added to previous space ,hence the total carpet area of Bank  increased to 2514 sq.ft  from 08.02.2001 (Ref Pages
136/C).
                During scrutiny of the above cited file it is seen that in page no 109 to 116/C rent of the  OBC Bank was  calculated. And here rent
and penalty calculated from 4/92 to 1/2005 which calculated to a sum of Rs39,79,346.00. The amount whether deposited by bank may be
clarified.
Bank rent calculated with 10% hike in every 2 years.
08.02.2001= Rs15,084.00
08.02.2003=Rs16,592.00
08.02.2005=Rs18,251.00
08.02.2007=Rs20,076.00
08.02.2009=Rs22,084.00
08.02.2011=Rs24,292.00
08.02.2013=Rs26,722.00
08.02.2015=Rs29,394.00
 
Hence the rent from 01.04.2014 came to Rs26,722 and Rs29,394.00 from 08.02.2015. And the total rent for 1.4.14-31.3.15 comes to
Rs.325339/-.
Again at 319/C regarding ATM, it was mentioned that the ATM installed in 18.09.2007 covering 93sqft carpet area which rent is Rs50.00 per
sqft and 10% hike in every 2 year. The details of rent are given below.
 
18.09.2007=Rs4,650.00
 117 / 124

[page 118]
 AUDIT REPORT 
27-06-2017
18.09.2009=Rs5,115.00
18.09.2011=Rs5,626.00
18.09.2013=Rs6,189.00
 
 The rent of ATM comes to Rs.74268/- for the year 2014-15.
But during course of checking of the file no AL-II-32/94 page 437 to 446/C it was seen that for 2014-15, Bank has been deposited rent
@12,246.00 per month for bank occupied space and Rs4,185.00 per month for ATM occupied space. But Bank rent for 2014-15 came to
Rs26,772 for 2/14 to 1/15 and Rs29,394.00 for 2/15 to 2/15 and Rs6,189.00 for ATM occupied space for the period 2014-15. So the total rent
of bank and ATM comes to Rs.399607/- against which collection has been made of Rs.197172/- making a less collection of Rs.202345-
only for the year 2014-15 . Similar less calculation has been done for all the previous years. In this way why the  local authority had been
losing a lump sum amount which may be clarified to audit.
Bank was always requested for lease deed of ATM and Bank.
    Audit objection memo in this regard issued to the local authority to clarify the following matter            
(1) why agreement for bank and ATM has not been done and if done the agreement may be produced to audit .
(2) whether the arrear dues with interest @16% are deposited by bank if deposited those may be shown to audit
(3) why rent collection has not been done at 10% hike rate in every 2 year as per the order. 
During exit conference the local authority furnished the following reply.
         Oriental Bank of Commerce was allotted 790 sft office space since 10.04.1992. Subsequently 782 sft. additional space was allotted w.e.f.
08.02.2001 and further 93 Sft was allotted for installation of ATM counter w.e.f. 01.04.2008.
            Initially, the rent for office space was fixed Rs.6/- per sft. and Rs.50/- per sft. for ATM counter. No agreement has not been executed
with OBC for the  above allotment.  BDA pursued to OBC for hike of of rent as per letter issued vide No. 4479/AL dtd. 12.07.2000 and deposit
interest on default payment.
 Previously the OBC Bank has paid for office space on 1549 sft.  and ATM counter for 93 sft. A joint measurement was held on 29.10.2011 by
the officials of BDA & OBC and finally the area calculated that OBC allotted total 1551 sft. for office space and 93 sft. for ATM counter.
         BDA has already revised the rent of Rs. 30/- per sft. for 1551 sft of  Office space i.e. Rs.46530/- P.M. exciuding Service Tax & Rs.90/- per
sft. for 93 sft. i.e. Rs.8370/- P.M.  excluding Service Tax of ATM counter w.e.f. 01.09.2016 which has been communicated to them vide Letter
No. 21508 dtd.19.07.16. The licence fee fixed will be enhanced by 10% at the interval of every two years. If there is default in payment the
licencee will be charged interest @ 18% per annum on the defaulted amount for the period of default. The proposal has been accepted by the
OBC Bank. BDA has also supplied a vetted Agreement format to OBC in this Office Letter No. 11762 dtd. 12.05.17 for submission to B.D.A.
after due signature of Authorised Signatory of the Bank for execution.
But due to lack of responsibility of the authorities the institution faces a loss of Rs.202435/-for the year 2014-15.
Hence amount less collected during the year under audit  is suggested for recovery,

18.5 - Production of SD/EMD/ISD/Misc. Deposit Ledger etc.Division-I OSP 59-64
On checking & scrutinizing the paid vouchers of Division-I, BDA, with reference to the Cash Book for the year 2014-15, it is noticed that a sum
of Rs.8654794.00 has been paid towards release of SD/EMD/ISD/Misc. deposit etc.As per Appendix -XXI of OPWD Code-Vol - II at Sl no. 19,
 118 / 124

[page 119]
 AUDIT REPORT 
27-06-2017
the register of securities should be maintained permanently. Hence, the SD/EMD/ISD/Misc. deposit ledger, concerned files & other connected
records may be produced to audit for verification of the genuineness of such payment.
In response to the audit objection memo the local authority replied with reference to the subject this is to state that EMD/ ISD received from the
contractors/ suppliers along with tender are entered in interest bearing security register maintained at division level and when the EMD/ISD are
refunded after completion of works and payment of final bills the same is refunded to the agencies on proper acknowledgement and this
registered hereby produced before local fund audit.Regarding SD/Misc deposit this is to state that the contractor bills are reflected in the cash
book and when the same are refunded to the parties a note is kept in the cash book showing the fact  of refund in the cash book under the
dated initials of Executive Engineer. Moreover the concerned vouchers where in the details of recoveries and refunds of SD/Misc deposit are
recorded have already been shown to LFA was verified the same. 
Register of security has not been maintained by the Local Authority towards deduction and refund of SD/Misc deposit though it has been
categorically mentioned vide appendix-XXI of OPWD code, Vol-II, Sl No-19 to maintain the same record, permanently, SD/Misc Deposits
deducted are only reflected in the cash book. A note is kept in the cash book only reflecting the fact of refund. As such the exact position of the
same deposit could not be ascertained. Hence the Local Authority is requested to maintain the same record and produce to next audit.

in favour of respective agencies of the different works this is to state that as per prevailing practice of Div-IV as well as other sister division of BDA
the security deposit are being refunded to the respective agencies on expiry of defect liability after approval of executive engineer and DAO in the

Regarding refund of EMD in favour of the respective agencies after approval of EE and DAO the EMD has been refunded to the concerned
agencies after completion of the work having recommendation submitted by the site engineer.

PARA: 19 AUDIT OF LOAN/DEPOSITS/CPF INCLUDING POSITIONS
19.1 - Govt Dues
As per Rule – 6 of OTC Vol – I read with Rule – 4 of O .G. F .R stipulates that all moneys received/realized on behalf of Govt. should be
deposited in treasury/ with the competent authority within 3 days of its receipt/realization. Retention of Govt. money/revenue outside the
treasury is irregular & not permissible. Scrutiny the cash books of divisions of B.D.A it was seen that amounts collected from the work bills
towards Rayalty, Vat, IT, Cess etc. during the year 2014-15 were deposited to Govt. a/c in the next month.Due to non availability of opening
balance, the balance position as on 31.3.2015 could not be furnished. However, the collection & payment of Royalty, Vat, IT, Cess during the
year 2014-15 is furnished below. Appropriate action need be taken to remit the balance amount of Govt dues in proper quarter.
